Specs: `reconcileOrderDraftInvoice — split order (basis:order) sibling-aware` (4 cases: no-double-bill, edit-added-line billed on base, single-group→legacy, finalized→legacy bail); (c) when the clean-partition guard bails (operator partials / a finalized invoice on the same line) the split order bills full ordered qty (conservative over-bill on a short-pick, never a double). **R1 post-delivery edit resync:** new **`resyncOrderInvoicesForEdit(orderId, tx?)`** (called by `updateOrderItems` for OFD/PARTIALLY_DELIVERED/DELIVERED edits, in its own `tenantTransaction`) rebuilds the order's FINALIZED / PAID / delivery-batch invoice(s) IN PLACE at **basis "order"** (the edited order qty — the only basis that survives the replace-all edit paths, which reset `deliveredQty`; correct for fully-delivered/OFD, and a documented "edit re-bills the edited qty" semantic for the short-pick case). Maps each order line → the invoice already billing it (via `InvoiceItem.orderItemId`), assigns NEW/unprovenanced lines to the **primary** invoice (the lone open draft, else earliest), and rebuilds via the shared **`rebuildSiblingDrafts(..., {preserveStatus:true})`** — which now, when `preserveStatus`, KEEPS payments and sets `status = recomputeStatus(Σ non-VOID payments, newTotal, dueDate, currentStatus)` instead of forcing DRAFT (an open draft stays DRAFT/terminal; a paid invoice shows the new balance: up→PARTIAL, down→PAID-overpaid). Re-syncs the ledger + resets each billed line's `invoicedQty` (removed/cancelled lines cleared to 0). **BAILS (null, writes nothing)** if a line is billed by two invoices, or any invoice line has no `orderItemId` provenance (never double-bills). Specs: `invoices.service.spec` `resyncOrderInvoicesForEdit` (8 cases: null-no-invoice, SENT rebuild+keep-payment→PARTIAL, PAID edited-down→PAID, VOID-payment-excluded, new-line-on-primary, ledger re-sync, 2 bails) + `orders.service.spec` (DELIVERED edit routes to resync, no revert, no throw-on-payment).
- **F05 — driver at-door money truth and run settlement (B49/B83/B148/B152/B167, PR #TBD).** Five changes that share one cause: the run payload never carried the billed line money, so every driver screen re-derived it. (1) **G7 — `RUN_LINE_ITEMS_SELECT`** (`routes.service.ts`, declared directly above `RUN_STOP_INCLUDE`) is now the ONLY run-read lineItems select: one const referenced by `RUN_STOP_INCLUDE`, `findOneRun`'s main query and its unlinked-orders fallback (three copies of the same literal before), carrying `subtotal`/`boxes`/`pieces`/`unitsPerBox` on top of the old six fields. **F10/F11/F12/F22 consume this const — extend it, never re-inline it.** `getRunPackingList`'s narrower include-only shape is deliberately NOT part of it. (2) **`recordDeliveryPaymentInTx` no longer discards the residual** — see the bullet above: it returns `excess`/`advancePaymentId` and books over-collection as an `AdvancePayment` (single-customer guarded; `reference` = **`RUN:<runId>:STOP:<stopId>`, a load-bearing token** `getRunCashCollections` matches by prefix). The zero-payable-invoice path (all invoices already PAID — the post-`reopenStop` re-collection case) used to drop the WHOLE amount and now advances it. (3) **`RunDeliveryDto.productId`** — the mobile payload always sent it and the global `forbidNonWhitelisted` pipe 400'd **every** stop completion; the DTO now accepts it and both `deliveryMutation.create` sites persist it (previously null, which also left `reopenStop`'s `mutation.productId`-gated stock restore dead). (4) **Server cash truth** — private `getRunCashCollections(runId, startedAt?)` sums CONFIRMED (`invoices/payment-predicates` `CONFIRMED_PAYMENT`) CASH/CHECK `InvoicePayment`s via `invoice.order.routeRunId` windowed on `paidAt >= startedAt`, plus RUN-referenced advances; surfaced as `collectedPayments` on `findOneRun` always and on `findAllRuns`/`findMyRuns` **only when driver-scoped** (batched over the page's runIds — operator-wide lists skip it deliberately). (5) **Settlement** — `POST /route-runs/:id/settlement` (`SettleRunDto`, a NEW endpoint: `PATCH :id` takes an unvalidated inline body that shipped mobile builds still use). Server computes expected itself and never trusts a client total; `varianceReason` required past a 1¢ tolerance; writes the dedicated `settlementNote`/`settlementVariance` columns (F01) — **never `run.notes`**, whose only renderer is hidden once a run closes (B167). DRIVER settles once and must own the run; OPERATOR may amend. Accepts IN_PROGRESS/COMPLETED/**CANCELLED** (a run cancelled mid-route still has cash in the truck — refusing would strand it), refuses SCHEDULED. **Three gates, because one was not enough:** `updateRunStatus` refuses COMPLETED while unsettled physical money exists, AND the **RF-016 auto-complete branches in both `completeStop` and `completeWithPayment`** apply the same predicate — they flip the run COMPLETED inside their own tx and bypass `updateRunStatus` entirely, so on the common path (last stop closed with cash in hand) the `updateRunStatus` guard alone never fires. ⚠️ **The CANCEL path and `deleteRun` are still ungated — handed to F11** (`Run cancel and skip reconciliation`), which owns those transitions.
- **F10 — reopenStop and stop-state guards (B54/B55/B71/B72/B120/B121, PR #TBD).** Six register bugs, one seam: the stop/run state machine mutated money-, stock- and compliance-bearing state without guarding WHERE a transition came from or WHO made it. (1) **B54 — reopen's payment guard read a dead model.** The `transaction.findMany` check could never fire (repo-wide there is **no** `.transaction.create` writer; the live at-door writer is `InvoicePayment` via `recordDeliveryPaymentInTx`), so a paid delivery reopened freely, stranding a PAID invoice on an order the system then called undelivered — and the re-collection went unrecorded because `PAYABLE` excludes PAID. `reopenStop` now also reads `invoice.findFirst({ orderId: { in: orderIds }, OR: [status in PAID/PARTIAL, payments: { some: CONFIRMED_PAYMENT }] })` and 400s **before** the transaction opens; **the legacy guard STAYS** (historical rows must still block). The predicate is the shared `invoices/payment-predicates` `CONFIRMED_PAYMENT`, never a literal — same rule as F03/F05. (2) **B55 — the stock loop was a double-credit, deleted.** See the `reopenStop` note above: delivery writes no stock, so there was nothing to reverse; the loop permanently inflated `currentStock` and minted uncosted positive SALE rows into COGS. Its removal makes the conservation rule uniform: **creation decrements with no movement row, delivery writes nothing, reopen writes nothing.** ⚠️ Historical damage is enumerable ONLY via the pre-fix rows (`type:SALE`, `quantity>0`, `reference LIKE 'Reopen stop %'`) — that reference prefix is now load-bearing for the D4 repair flight; never reuse it for a new writer. (3) **B71 — `updateStopStatus` had no from-state guard at all**, so a COMPLETED stop could be PATCHed straight to SKIPPED, blinding `orders.service.ts`'s demotion guard (which keys on `stop.status === "COMPLETED"`). It now 409s on a COMPLETED stop and on any transition under a COMPLETED/CANCELLED run, and loads the run for **every** caller (previously driver-only). (4) **B72 — `forbiddenRunTransition(from,to)`** in `dto/update-run-status.dto.ts` is a deliberate **deny-list, not a whitelist**: only CANCELLED→COMPLETED and COMPLETED→SCHEDULED are refused, same→same stays legal (idempotent retries). ⚠️ **CANCELLED is deliberately NOT absorbing** (narrowed in review): the un-cancel (CANCELLED→SCHEDULED/IN_PROGRESS) is the ONLY recovery an accidentally cancelled run has — `deleteRun` refuses any run carrying a `deliveryMutation`, `reopenStop` refuses a cancelled run, and the dispatch sweep needs `routeRunStopId: null`, which F11's `releaseUndeliveredOrders` now clears inside the cancel transaction — see the F11 bullet below. WHO may un-cancel is gated in `updateRunStatus`, not in the matrix: a DRIVER caller on a CANCELLED run gets `ForbiddenException("This run was cancelled — ask your operator to restart it.")`, so a stale device cannot resurrect a called-off run by replaying "start run". Censused first — web sends only CANCELLED, mobile sends IN_PROGRESS/COMPLETED, and an operator SCHEDULED→COMPLETED backfill is spec-pinned; a whitelist would have broken all three. COMPLETED→IN_PROGRESS stays allowed **deliberately**. Ownership: DRIVER callers must match `run.driverId` in `updateRunStatus`, `completeStop` **and** `completeWithPayment` — the latter two fetched the driver row and never compared it, and the check now sits before POD ingest and before the tx opens, so a hijacker's capture is never stored. (5) **B120 — reopen nulled the only pointers to stored POD artifacts** (`signatureUrl`/`podPhotoUrls` are storage keys since #477) with no deletion anywhere, orphaning regulated-delivery evidence. It now writes an `AuditLog` row (`action: "route_stop.reopened"`, entityType `RouteRunStop`) carrying the discarded capture **inside the same transaction** — evidence stays recoverable and the objects stay referenced; storage is deliberately NOT deleted. That row is also the forward-looking reopen trail the pre-fix era lacked. The same reset also appends `{archivedAt, signatureUrl, podPhotoUrls, reason:"reopen"}` to `RouteRunStop.podHistory` — the column F01 declared for exactly this write: the audit row is the who/when trail, `podHistory` is the stop-local archive a read surface joins to without an AuditLog query (nothing is appended when a reopen displaces no pointers, e.g. a SKIPPED stop). (6) **B121 — `attachPodArtifact` took no user at all** and overwrote `signatureUrl` unconditionally for any new artifactId, so any in-tenant caller could substitute a completed regulated stop's compliance signature months later. It now takes `user: JwtPayload` (controller passes `@CurrentUser`), applies the driver-run binding **before** the idempotent read, and 409s a `kind:"signature"` write on a COMPLETED stop that already has one. Photos stay appendable and the same-artifactId offline replay still returns the stored artifact. Its sibling READ `getStopPod` (`GET .../pod`) takes `user` and carries the same binding — it presigns the signature and every POD photo, and `RouteRun.driverId` is mutable via `updateRun`, so an unbound read left a reassigned driver pulling that evidence forever while the write was blocked. **NOT F10's:** `deleteRun`/`deleteRoute` gates remain open.
- **F11 — run cancel / skip reconciliation (B129/B211/B146/B34, PR #TBD).** `routes.service.ts`:
  `releaseUndeliveredOrders(tx, stopIds)` (private; OUT_FOR_DELIVERY→CONFIRMED revert, then null
  `routeRunId`/`routeRunStopId` for orders on the given stops with status ∉ {DELIVERED, CANCELLED},
  then DECLINE those orders' PENDING `ChangeRequest`s; empty input writes nothing). ⚠️ **Every
  order write carries `routeRunStop: { status: { not: "COMPLETED" } }`** — the caller's stop read
  and these writes are separate statements under READ COMMITTED, and a driver's
  `completeWithPayment` committing in that window leaves a COMPLETED stop with a
  `PARTIALLY_DELIVERED` order that the `notIn` list does NOT exclude; the relation filter is what
  keeps that order's at-door cash inside `getRunCashCollections` and out of the trip builder. Never
  add `PARTIALLY_DELIVERED` to the `notIn` instead — a partially-delivered order on a genuinely
  open stop must still release. Because `updateMany` returns no ids, the unlink's row set is read
  first (`order.findMany`, same predicate) and the unlink is then keyed by those ids too, so the
  released set and the declined set are provably the same rows. The CR decline writes
  `status: DECLINED`, `resolution: "DECLINED"`, `resolutionReason: RELEASED_CHANGE_REQUEST_REASON`
  (exported from `routes.service.ts`), `resolvedAt` — **resolver identity stays null** (system
  decline) and **no notification fires** (`notifyRequester` is a follow-up row). Rationale: a CR is
  only created and only applied while `order.routeRun.status === IN_PROGRESS`, so a released order
  is back in the buyer's direct-edit window (`updateOrderItems` has no pending-CR check) while its
  CR sits PENDING — on re-dispatch the driver approves it and the items apply twice. ⚠️
  `scripts/repair-f11-stranded-orders.mjs` does the same unlink for pre-fix rows and now declines
  too (see its runbook section). called inside a `tenantTransaction` that now wraps the
  CANCELLED and COMPLETED writes of `updateRunStatus` (in-tx read of non-COMPLETED stop ids) and
  one line after the RF-016 `tx.routeRun.update` in `completeStop`/`completeWithPayment`
  (`id !== stopId && status !== COMPLETED`). Every call site LOGS its released count (an
  unlogged release leaves a null pointer indistinguishable from a never-dispatched order).
  SCHEDULED/IN_PROGRESS (incl. un-cancel) stay a bare update — un-cancel re-pins nothing and is
  NOT a path back to the released orders: `createRun` refuses a route that already carries a
  SCHEDULED/IN_PROGRESS run, so re-dispatch = cancel again, then dispatch a fresh run
  (follow-up row). `reopenStop` refuses `stop SKIPPED ∧ run COMPLETED` — the REFUSAL is that
  state pair alone, but the MESSAGE branches on the RELEASE HELPER'S OWN predicate — does the
  stop still hold an order with status ∉ {DELIVERED, CANCELLED}: no ⇒ 400 'released to
  dispatch'; yes (a pre-deploy row the D4 repair has not reached) ⇒ 400 'still attached … F11
  stranded-order repair', because for that data nothing was released and the trip builder cannot
  see the orders. NOT `stop.orders.length` — the helper leaves DELIVERED/CANCELLED orders pinned,
  so a post-deploy release can hand the stop back still holding some, and those are exactly the
  rows `repair-f11-stranded-orders.mjs` (same status filter) would skip. `findOneRun`'s legacy
  fallback is skipped for a CANCELLED run, and for a COMPLETED run **only when some stop is not
  COMPLETED** — an all-COMPLETED-stop COMPLETED run was never released by F11 (the helper only
  ever sees non-COMPLETED stop ids), so its empty `orders` arrays are the genuine legacy/seeded
  shape the fallback serves and it keeps its backfill (demo-seed's `past-1`/`past-2` runs are
  exactly that). An un-cancelled fully-released run still reaches it — follow-up row. ⚠️ **`Order.routeRunId` has TWO report readers and the release moves BOTH, by
  design (spec R15):** office-recorded CASH/CHECK on a released order leaves the run's expected
  figure (`getRunCashCollections`), and `bookkeeping.service.ts` `getSalesByDriver` (invoice →
  order → routeRun → driver, `if (!driver) continue`) drops EVERY invoice on a released order —
  payment or not — out of Sales-by-Driver until a re-dispatch re-pins it, at which point the
  credit follows the driver who actually delivers. Called out in the PR body and in
  `scripts/REPAIR-RUNBOOK.md`'s F11 section (the D4 repair makes it retroactive).
  **Sweep extraction was proposed and DROPPED by the lead** —
  `createRun`'s post-lock order sweep at :936-967 stays byte-for-byte untouched (un-cancel is
  status-only, so the sweep has no second caller); the "SCHEDULED dispatch with no orderIds" pin
  still asserts its `where` deep-equal. `dto/update-run-status.dto.ts` doc comment rewritten for
  the release (was: "a terminal CANCELLED would strand the run AND its undelivered orders").
  D4 repair for pre-fix stranded rows: `scripts/repair-f11-stranded-orders.mjs`. Specs:
  `routes.service.run-terminal-release{,.pins}.spec.ts` (T27 = the read→write race, T28 = the CR
  decline). Lesson **L-045**.
- **route-optimization controller** `route-optimization` — `:id/optimize`, `:id/analyze` (external optimizer suggests stop order). **2026-08-27 route-planning batch:** optimizer is cost-matrix-based (`cost-matrix.ts` `buildCostMatrices` — Google computeRouteMatrix element-array parse, LRU cache, `routes-billing` telemetry, haversine fallback; generalized `solveOrder` NN+2-opt honors Route.optimizeBy TIME|DISTANCE, avoidTolls, optional fixed END; ORS demoted to no-key path) + `POST /routes/:id/variants` (FASTEST/SHORTEST/NO_TOLLS, hasTolls by contrast, ≤10-intermediate polyline cap, never-500) + `POST :id/variants/apply` (`dto/apply-route-variant.dto.ts`; optional `runId` re-numbers run stops in-transaction; SOLE `plannedPolyline` writer — all other reorder paths NULL it). Route schema += origin/end/tolls/metric/plannedPolyline (migration `20260907000000`). Trips: `resolveEnd` + `PATCH /trips/routes/:routeId/planning` (`dto/route-planning.dto.ts`; 409 on IN_PROGRESS run). `POST /routes` create resolves origin/end server-side too. Run payload route selects (findOneRun/findMyRuns/driver list) widened with depot*/end*. Full detail: CHANGELOG.md 2026-08-27 bullet.
- **`route-analysis.service.ts` metering (2026-08-28):** `analyzeRoute`/`analyzeRouteRun` record an `AiUsageEvent` (`insights.route`, model from `resolveModel()`) right after each successful model call — a later parse failure still books the real spend exactly once (`usageRecorded` flag); a failed call books `success:false` (model `"unknown"` if it died before `resolveModel`). No-key remains a soft `configured:false` with zero rows. Spec: `route-analysis.service.spec.ts`.
- side effects: Route/RouteStop/RouteCustomer/RouteRun/RouteRunStop/DeliveryMutation writes; Socket.io broadcast; payment recording; inventory adjust on mutations.
- **Ad-hoc trips (2026-08-24, `order_delivery`-gated, see `trips/` below):** `Route.kind RouteKind @default(SCHEDULED)` (migration `20260904000000_adhoc_trips_and_fulfillment`, additive, `@@index([tenantId, kind])`) — unforgeable via the public routes API (`CreateRouteDto`/`UpdateRouteDto` carry no `kind` field; only `trips.service.ts` writes `kind: ADHOC`). `findAllRoutes` defaults its filter to `kind: SCHEDULED` (ships before any ADHOC row can exist, so existing callers/UI see no behavior change) and `getCustomerRouteAssignments` filters to SCHEDULED only. **2026-08-25 (Deliveries history list):** `findAllRoutes`'s `include` additionally selects `_count: {runs, stops}` and the single latest run (`runs: {orderBy: createdAt desc, take: 1, select: {id, status, scheduledDate, completedAt, driver: {id, contactName}}}`) so web's `/deliveries` page can render Date/Driver/Stops/Status without a per-row round-trip; purely additive to the response shape — filtering, pagination and the SCHEDULED default are byte-unchanged (existing `routes.service.spec.ts` cases still pass). **`updateRoute` updates scalars only** — stops are mutated exclusively through the separate add/reorder/remove endpoints above, each active-run-guarded; there is no "replace-all stops" path here (confirmed absent from the ad-hoc trips feature — see `docs/phase0-adhoc-trips-findings.md`). The dispatch sweep's where now also pins **`fulfillPath: FulfillPath.ROUTE`** — SHIP orders never attach to a run stop, on SCHEDULED routes as well as ad-hoc trips (otherwise a driver would see a carrier-shipped order on their route and could complete the stop / take a delivery payment against it). Existing rows are all ROUTE (column default, never backfilled), so live dispatch behaviour is unchanged; the sweep where-object is deep-equal spec-pinned. `createRun`'s `orderIds` param: 400 on a non-ADHOC route before any writes; on ADHOC it narrows the sweep by ONLY adding `id: { in: orderIds }` on top of the existing `customerId`/`routeRunStopId: null`/`fulfillPath` filters (conditional-spread pattern); ADHOC called WITHOUT `orderIds` is a 400 before any writes (a draft trip stores no order linkage, so an un-narrowed sweep would attach every open order of each stop's customer — only the trip builder knows the picked set; web `routes/templates/[id]` and mobile `(operator)/routes/[id]` hide their Dispatch action for ADHOC accordingly). Active-run 409 guard unchanged. `createRun` returns the run plus **`attachedOrderCount`** (sum of the sweep's `updateMany` counts) so a caller can detect orders that changed between building a trip and sending it and were silently left off. **`deleteRoute` refuses an ADHOC route whose run is IN_PROGRESS or COMPLETED (400)** — the cascade nulls `Order.routeRunId`/`routeRunStopId` and hard-deletes every `RouteRunStop` (POD photos, signature, arrival/completion times) while `DeliveryBatch`/`DeliveryMutation.routeRunStopId` are `ON DELETE SET NULL`, so the delete would succeed and silently orphan the delivery record; an ad-hoc trip is the ONLY delivery record its orders have. Drafts and scheduled/cancelled runs (nothing recorded yet) stay deletable, and SCHEDULED routes are untouched by the guard. Both trip lists render the delete affordance only for run-less drafts.

### `trips/` (ad-hoc order trips, 2026-08-24, `order_delivery`-gated — NEW module)

- **Addon-gated 2026-08-28:** class-level `@UseGuards(JwtAuthGuard, RolesGuard, AddonGuard)` + `@RequireAddon("order_delivery", "developer_mode")` — order_delivery-only, unlike the shared `/routes`+`/drivers` either-gate. **Exception:** `PATCH /trips/routes/:routeId/planning` re-declares the either-gate at handler level (it is saved from the shared run-detail page); handler metadata wins in `getAllAndOverride`. `trips.module.ts` gained `BillingModule`.
- **controller** `trips` `@Roles(OPERATOR)` — `GET /trips/eligibility` (query orderIds) + `POST /trips` (build+dispatch), both sharing ONE `checkEligibility` predicate. **NEW 2026-08-26 batch-d: `GET /trips/eligible-orders`** (search/page/limit/exclude; `dto/eligible-orders.dto.ts`) — paginated CANDIDATE list for the builder's in-page order picker; final verdict per row still comes from the same `checkEligibility`, ineligible rows filtered server-side so the picker can never disagree with `POST /trips`.
- **service** `TripsService` — **`checkEligibility(order)`**: eligible = `status ∈ {PENDING, CONFIRMED, PARTIALLY_DELIVERED}` ∧ `fulfillPath: ROUTE` ∧ `routeRunStopId: null` ∧ ≥1 `CustomerAddress` row; a non-null `routeRunStopId` distinguishes `ON_ACTIVE_RUN` (run SCHEDULED/IN_PROGRESS, includes run/driver detail) from `PREVIOUSLY_DISPATCHED`; `NO_ADDRESS` only fires on zero address rows. **F11:** since a terminal run releases its undelivered orders (`routes.service.ts releaseUndeliveredOrders` nulls the pointer at cancel/complete time), `PREVIOUSLY_DISPATCHED` now marks pre-fix stranded rows only, freed by `scripts/repair-f11-stranded-orders.mjs`. **`groupOrdersForTrip`** (imported from the local mirror `common/trip-grouping.ts`, NOT from `@routeflow/types` — see that file's header + the `no-runtime-workspace-imports.spec.ts` guard; the shared original lives at `packages/types/trip-grouping.ts`, also mirrored at `apps/mobile/lib/trip-grouping.ts`) groups by `customerId` into one stop per distinct customer, tie-broken by `isDefault desc, createdAt asc` when picking the address; orders with no customer land in `skipped[]` with reason `NO_CUSTOMER`. **`resolveOrigin(dto)`** — three tiers: `DRIVER` (400 naming the driver if no `homeLat`/`homeLng`), `ADDRESS` (geocodes via the shared `common/geocode.util.ts`; a geocode failure 400s and **`route.create` is never called** — the HTTP geocode call happens OUTSIDE the create transaction so a bad address can't leave a half-built route), `TENANT` (mirrors `routes.service.ts` `resolveDepot` tiers 2-3 — tenant depot address/coords). `POST /trips`: any ineligible order → 409 listing `{orderId, orderNumber, customerName, reason, detail}` per order (whole-request all-or-nothing, no partial trip); `dto.driverId` (the trip's assigned driver) is tenant-checked with the same `driver.findFirst({ id, tenantId })` + 404 as `resolveOrigin`'s DRIVER tier, so a cross-tenant driver can't be pinned onto the route/run FK; on success creates exactly ONE `Route` (`kind: ADHOC`, depot\* = resolved origin, one stop per distinct customer, `stopNumber` 1..n) and performs **ZERO** writes to any `Order` row (orders keep `fulfillPath: ROUTE`/`routeRunStopId: null` until a later `POST /route-runs` dispatch — trip creation is purely a routing/grouping step, not a dispatch).
- side effects: Route/RouteStop writes only (no Order/RouteRun writes — dispatch is the existing `createRun` flow above, unchanged).

### `invoices/`

- **controller** `invoices` — from-order(+/partial), payments (export/record/get/list), per-id: get/patch, send, send-email, send-reminder, void, revert-to-draft, unvoid, reopen, duplicate, pdf, write-off, payments CRUD, price-adjustment, delete, `PATCH :id/payments/:paymentId/check-status` (OPERATOR, P5-12).
- **Order-date dating rule + payment settlement (2026-08-07):** every FROM-ORDER invoice path now derives `issueDate = order.orderDate ?? new Date()` and anchors `dueDate = issueDate + termDays` (it used to anchor to _now_, so a backdated invoice was born with a future-relative due date) — the three sites are `createInvoiceFromOrder`'s `extraInvoiceData` into `createSplitInvoices`, `createInvoiceFromOrderWithTenant`, and `createPartialFromOrder`. The regulated ledger follows for free (`soldAt = invoice.issueDate` ⇒ `periodBucket` lands in that PAST month — owner-accepted restatement of re-generated regulatory reports). **`resyncInvoiceLedger` is deliberately untouched**: its reversals must keep booking in the CURRENT period (explicit never-backdate rule). `resyncOrderInvoicesForEdit` still never touches `issueDate`. — **`InvoicePayment.settledAt`** (migration `20260807000200_...`, `@@index([tenantId, settledAt])`) is when the money actually LANDED in the bank, vs `paidAt` = when the instrument was received; it MAY be a FUTURE date (post-dated checks) and nothing clamps it. Distinct from `clearedAt`, which stays a server-stamped CHECK-lifecycle audit mark. Accepted on Record/Update/Standalone/SetCheckStatus DTOs; `updatePayment` distinguishes key-absent (preserve) from explicit `null` (clear); `recordStandalonePayment` writes it across every row of the `paymentGroupId` group; `setCheckStatus` CLEARED lets an explicit date win (setting both `clearedAt` and `settledAt`) but otherwise **leaves `settledAt` alone** — `clearedAt` still defaults to the clock because it is the audit stamp of the transition, while `settledAt` is operator-supplied truth about the bank and is never inferred from when someone clicked. Defaulting it to now (as the first cut did) silently yanked an old check out of its original cash-basis period into the current one, restating already-reported numbers — and no shipped client can pass an explicit date yet, so that was the only reachable behaviour. Driver at-door `recordDeliveryPaymentInTx` stays unchanged (settlement unknown at the door). `listAllPayments`/`exportPayments` sort on it; CSV gained a `Bank Date` column.
- **service** — `findAll`, `findOne`, `createFromOrder`, `update`, `send`, `sendEmail`, `sendReminder`, `void`, `revertToDraft`, `unvoid`, `reopen`, `duplicate`, `generatePDF`, `recordPayment`, `voidPayment`, `priceAdjustment`, `writeOff`, `setCheckStatus` (P5-12).
- **Order-level discount carried onto generated invoices (2026-08-25, sale-integrity phase 2 WP2, money):** `createSplitInvoices` previously never read `order.discountAmount`, so a fully-discounted order ($20 subtotal, $20 discount, $0 total) generated an invoice for the full $20 — over-billing. Each `groupData` entry now gets a `discount` field, allocated proportionally by group subtotal EXACTLY like the existing regular-tax allocator just above it in the same function (`roundMoney` each share, the largest-subtotal group absorbs the rounding remainder via the same recipient rule) — never a new scheme. `gd.total` composition changed from `subtotal + taxAmount + shippingFee` to `subtotal + taxAmount + shippingFee − discount`, and the invoice create payload's `discount: 0` literal became `discount: gd.discount`. A zero/null `discountAmount` allocates nothing to every group (`orderDiscount > 0` guard), so the create payloads stay BYTE-IDENTICAL to before this allocator existed (deep-equal spec). `reconcileOrderDraftInvoice`/`createPartialFromOrder` are untouched — only `createSplitInvoices` changed. Specs: `invoices.service.spec` single-group full-discount (20/20 → discount 20, total 0), split two-group order (discount 15 → shares sum to exactly 15, Σ sibling totals == order total), zero-discount byte-equality.
- **Atomic SENT-flip + credit auto-apply (P5-13, money-critical)** — `send()` and `sendEmail()`'s "mark SENT" step are now ONE `Serializable` tenantTransaction: the `status→SENT` update + `CreditNotesService.autoApplyOldestCreditsInTx(tx, id, customerId)` (oldest-first, non-expired, running-balance clamp) commit together; the emitted socket event + returned invoice reflect `auto.invoiceStatus ?? SENT`. Idempotent — a re-send finds the balance covered or credits exhausted and applies nothing. `sendReminder` does NOT flip status and is untouched. New constructor dep `CreditNotesService` (`InvoicesModule` imports `CreditNotesModule` — acyclic, no `forwardRef`).
- **Check lifecycle (P5-12) — money-critical:** fields live on **`InvoicePayment`** (NOT `Payment`/`Transaction`, which are dead code — nothing ever writes them): `checkStatus CheckStatus?` (`RECORDED|DEPOSITED|CLEARED|BOUNCED`), `depositedAt/clearedAt/bouncedAt DateTime?`, `nsfFeeAmount Decimal?`. Migration `20260724000000_add_check_lifecycle` (additive: enum + 5 nullable cols + RECORDED backfill for existing non-VOID CHECK payments). `recordPayment`/`recordStandalonePayment` (invoices.service.ts) and `BookkeepingService.recordPayment` all stamp `checkStatus: method==="CHECK" ? RECORDED : null` at create time. `setCheckStatus(invoiceId,paymentId,dto)` — forward-only transition map `CHECK_TRANSITIONS` (RECORDED→DEPOSITED→CLEARED strict; any non-BOUNCED→BOUNCED; BOUNCED terminal). DEPOSITED/CLEARED: stamp only, `PaymentStatus` stays PAID, no balance change. BOUNCED (NSF) mirrors the existing `voidPayment` pattern exactly: sets `InvoicePayment.status=VOID` (so every existing non-VOID paid-sum/`status:PAID` aggregation excludes it automatically), then optionally creates a non-taxable ad-hoc NSF `InvoiceItem` (`taxRate:0`) for `roundMoney(nsfFeeAmount)` and bumps STORED `Invoice.subtotal`/`total` by the same amount in the same `tenantTransaction` (consistent with the total invariant `total = subtotal − invDiscount + shipping + Σ item.subtotal×taxRate` since a taxRate:0 line only moves subtotal), then `recomputeStatus` re-opens the invoice. `findOne`'s `paidAmount` now filters `payments.status !== "VOID"` (fixed pre-existing void-blindness — a bounced check no longer counts as paid). `routes.service.ts` payment path / `PaymentMethod` / `PaymentStatus` enums deliberately untouched. `prisma-mock.ts` gained `paymentCounter: modelProxy()` in both `allModels()`/`txModels()`.
- **Invoice line code = unit code (2026-07-23, products-dual-sku WP3)** — new pure helper `invoice-item-code.ts` `invoiceItemCode(p?: {unitSku?,barcode?,sku?}|null): string|null` = `p?.unitSku ?? p?.barcode ?? p?.sku ?? null` (spec'd `invoice-item-code.spec.ts`, same style as `invoice-pdf-variant.ts`). `invoice-pdf.service.ts`'s item `product` select now includes `unitSku: true`; the Code128 `barcodeText` (feeds both the barcode image and its caption) is now `invoiceItemCode((item as any).product)` instead of the old inline `barcode ?? sku` — retailers scan the inner **unit** code, not the case code. Only this renderer changed: buyer statement PDF prints no product codes; tobacco report stays on `sku` (regulator/wholesaler-facing).
- **Invoice PDF DRAFT/FINAL stage** — `invoice-pdf.service.ts` `getOrGenerate(id,{force,variant?})` renders **fresh every call** (the "Generated `<date, time>`" footer stamp + stage marker must be current). `invoice-pdf-variant.ts` (pure, spec'd) `deriveInvoiceVariant({status,order?.status})` = **FINAL** once the invoice is issued (status≠DRAFT) or its order is `DELIVERED`, else **DRAFT** (the pre-delivery proforma). `invoice-pdf-template.tsx` renders a diagonal **DRAFT** watermark + red DRAFT badge (draft) / green FINAL badge (final) + the generated-at datetime footer; the Terms box now **wraps across pages** (long T&Cs never clip). Endpoints thread `variant`: `GET :id/pdf?variant=draft|final`, `POST :id/send-email {variant}` (→ `sendEmail(id,email,variant)`). Web invoice-detail Draft｜Final toggle drives Print/Download/Email; **mobile invoice-detail now mirrors it (WP-2)** — one `SegmentedControl` Draft｜Final toggle (`pdfVariantOverride ?? deriveInvoiceVariant(invoice)`) governs BOTH Share (`useInvoicePdf({variant})`) and Send (`useSendInvoice` now threads `variant` into the send-email body — NOT the bodyless `/send` mark-as-sent), replacing the old two fixed Share-draft/Share-final tiles; `AdminInvoice` gained `order.status` (was untyped). `deriveInvoiceVariant` is a **triple mirror** — `apps/api/src/invoices/invoice-pdf-variant.ts` (canonical) + a named `apps/web/lib/api/invoices.ts#deriveInvoiceVariant` (extracted from the inlined page expr) + `apps/mobile/lib/invoice-pdf-variant.ts` (spec `apps/mobile/__tests__/invoice-pdf-variant.test.ts`) — keep in sync. Any version, any time; smart default per stage. **`buildInvoiceItemData`** prorates boxed lines and carries an order line's override as net `unitPrice` + `originalPrice` (strikethrough) with **`discount: 0`** — it must NOT re-derive a discount from `originalPrice` or the override double-counts (every consumer bills `computeLineSubtotal(unitPrice) − discount`); **`recomputeOrderFromInvoices(orderId)`** = backward sync (rebuilds the linked order's items+totals from the SUM of all non-void invoices), called from `update`/`priceAdjustment` when `orderId` set (inverse of `reconcileOrderDraftInvoice`). side effects: Invoice/InvoiceItem/InvoicePayment + linked Order writes; Transaction ledger; email; PDF; journal entries.
- **MSRP snapshot on the invoice line (2026-08-22, PR-B — ⚠️ IN FLIGHT on `feat/msrp-on-invoices`, NOT on master):**
  `Product.msrp Decimal?` (universal default, **per PIECE** even for boxed lines),
  `CustomerPrice.msrp Decimal?` per-customer override **and `CustomerPrice.pricingTier` became
  `Int?`** so a row may now carry an MSRP override with no tier (migration
  `20260831000000_add_msrp_pricing`, additive + one NOT-NULL drop). ⚠️ **That nullable tier is
  the ripple risk** — every `getTierPrice(...)` caller must keep its `?? customer default`
  fallback; `Customer.pricingTier` itself is UNCHANGED (still `Int @default(1)`). `InvoiceItem.msrp
Decimal?` is the **snapshot taken at line creation**, resolved server-side by
  `applyMsrpSnapshots(db, customerId, itemsData)` (private, `invoices.service.ts`) → `common/msrp.ts`
  `loadMsrpMap`; it **no-ops unless the tenant has `flag.msrp`**, so an un-flagged tenant simply
  writes null. Called at every line-creating site: `create()` (which also covers recurring
  generation — `recurring-invoices.service` delegates to it), all four `buildInvoiceItemData`
  callers, `update()`'s DRAFT rebuild, and `estimates.service convertToInvoice` (a separate direct
  `InvoiceItem` writer that is easy to miss); `duplicate()` copies the value verbatim. **Render
  surfaces read the LINE, never the live product** — an issued invoice must not change when someone
  edits the product's MSRP later. Display-only, never money math; null renders blank, never `$0.00`.
  Rendered on four API-owned surfaces plus the two web ones: `invoice-pdf-template.tsx` (a second
  line under the Unit Price cell; the 4-column layout is unchanged) and `email.service.ts`
  `buildInvoiceEmail` (muted line under the unit price, fed by the item maps in
  `invoices.service.ts` `sendEmail` **and** `sendReminder` — both must pass `msrp` or the reminder
  silently drops it). ⚠️ **`invoice-pdf.service.ts` deliberately needs NO change and must not get
  one** — it loads items via `include`, so the column flows through automatically; adding `msrp` to
  a product `select` there would make the PDF re-read the LIVE product and historical invoices
  would start changing. Regression specs: `common/msrp.spec.ts` (resolver), `invoices.service.spec.ts`
  "MSRP snapshots" (flag-off no-op never queries; flag-on stamping, override precedence, productless
  lines untouched), and `orders.service.spec.ts` "an msrp-only CustomerPrice row" (a
  `{pricingTier: null, msrp}` row is pricing-inert — prices at the customer's default tier).
  ⚠️ `customers.service.ts upsertCustomerPrice` is now a PARTIAL update (only fields present in the
  DTO are touched; a row cleared of both fields is DELETED) and takes the caller's `JwtPayload`:
  the implicit delete is held to OPERATOR level (OPERATOR/TENANT_ADMIN/SUPER_ADMIN) because
  `POST /customers/:id/prices` also admits DRIVER while `DELETE /:id/prices/:priceId` is
  deliberately OPERATOR-only — without the gate a driver could erase a negotiated override by
  posting `{pricingTier: null}`. Specs: `customers.service.spec.ts` "upsertCustomerPrice".
  **B132 (F14 2026-09-02) — that gate covered only the IMPLICIT delete branch; a DRIVER could
  still POST a non-null `pricingTier`/`msrp` and write a real override, because `POST
:id/prices`'s `@Roles` decorator admitted DRIVER outright.** `customers.controller.ts` now
  declares `@Roles(OPERATOR)` on `upsertCustomerPrice`, matching `deleteCustomerPrice`'s roles
  exactly. Defense in depth: the role check inside `customers.service.ts upsertCustomerPrice` is
  now HOISTED to the top of the method (was only reached via the implicit-delete branch) and runs
  BEFORE any DTO validation or Prisma read — a non-operator caller must not learn whether an
  override exists, let alone change one. `user` is no longer optional on the method signature.
  Specs: `customers.controller.roles.spec.ts` (role-decorator parity), `customers.service.spec.ts`
  (inverted the old test that asserted a DRIVER MAY clear a tier — it had been pinning the bug).
- **Order→invoice shipping fee (2026-07-18):** from-order creation no longer hardcodes `shippingFee: 0` — `createSplitInvoices` seeds the REMAINING order fee (order fee − Σ existing non-void invoice fees) onto exactly ONE sibling (largest subtotal, same recipient as the tax remainder; never prorated ⇒ Σ sibling totals == order total; runs for tax-exempt too); partial-from-order gives the first partial the whole remainder. `reconcileOrderDraftInvoice` treats the ORDER as fee source of truth (draftFee = order fee − other-invoice fees, overwrites the draft). `rebuildSiblingDrafts` stability rule: keep per-sibling placement when Σ(sibling fees)==order fee, else re-seed onto largest sibling. `recomputeOrderFromInvoices` back-syncs `Order.shippingFee = Σ non-void invoice fees` into the order (+ fee-inclusive total) — this is what lets invoice-side fee edits survive order-driven reconciles. Specs: invoices.service.spec shipping-fee describe blocks.
- **Regulated invoice split (Phase 4 W4)** — `createInvoiceFromOrder`/`createInvoiceFromOrderWithTenant` now **return `Invoice[]`** (contract change; callers `createSale` sends every sibling + returns primary, web hook `useCreateInvoiceFromOrder` typed `Invoice[]`). New `groupOrderLinesForInvoicing` partitions lines by resolved category (`OrderItem.trackedCategoryId ?? product.trackedCategoryId`) → a **standard group** (uncategorised + non-`SEPARATE_INVOICE` treatments, which fold in + `logger.warn`) first, then one group per `SEPARATE_INVOICE` category (name-sorted). New `createSplitInvoices` does the money: per-group subtotal, regular tax allocated proportionally with the **largest group absorbing the rounding remainder** (Σ group regular tax == single-invoice tax exactly), category tax per group; numbering **base / -R1 / -R2** (one sequence number, derived suffixes), shared `invoiceGroupId` only when >1 group; bumps `invoicedQty` once/line. **Single-group orders are byte-identical to pre-W4.** `buildInvoiceItemData` snapshots `trackedCategoryId`+`categoryTaxAmount` onto the line (categoryTax prorated telescoping like subtotal). Order snapshot in `orders.service.create` (`OrderItem.trackedCategoryId` + `Order.hasRegulated`). **RF-4 (WIRED — interim rate>0 guard REMOVED):** category tax is now folded into totals everywhere. Shared helper **`foldCategoryTax(itemsData, isTaxExempt)`** = Σ per-line `categoryTaxAmount`, but returns 0 AND zeroes each line snapshot when `isTaxExempt` (exemption covers everything — $0 of BOTH taxes; ledger records $0). Applied at EVERY invoice-tax site: `createSplitInvoices` (per group), manual `create()`/`update()`/`duplicate()` (compute from the product's section), `reconcileOrderDraftInvoice` single-draft, `rebuildSiblingDrafts` (per draft), `createPartialFromOrder`. **Split invariant:** each order line lands in exactly one group so Σ group categoryTax == order categoryTax; combined with proportional regular tax ⇒ **Σ(sibling total) == order total** (subtotal + regular + category). `recomputeOrderFromInvoices` back-sync aggregates billed `categoryTaxAmount` per line → order line + order total. **DEFERRED:** `completeStop` route-delivery split, credit-note sibling reversal, products DTO `trackedCategoryId` wiring, PER_VOLUME volume-per-piece source (uses pieces).

### `credit-notes/`

- **controller** `credit-notes` — create, list, get, issue, apply, void.
- **service** — `create`, `findAll`, `findOne`, `issue`, `apply`, `void`. side effects: CreditNote writes; ledger entries; optional auto-apply to invoices.
- **Wallet (P5-13, money-critical)** — `CreditNote` += `expiresAt DateTime?`/`appliedAt DateTime?`/`autoApplied Boolean @default(false)` (migration `20260725000000_add_credit_wallet`, additive, after `20260724000000_add_check_lifecycle`). **Canonical open-credit predicate** (every wallet/auto-apply site must match): `status != VOID && (amount − amountUsed) > 0.001 && (expiresAt == null || expiresAt > now)` — "expired" is a computed FILTER, never a status flip, never claws back an `InvoicePayment`. `create()` accepts optional `expiresAt` ISO (validated in-service — no `CreateCreditNoteDto` class exists, controller takes `dto: any`; rejects non-date/past dates). **`applyCreditInTx(tx, cn, inv, requestedAmount?, opts?)`** — private, tx-safe (never opens its own tx), the single money core: `roundMoney`s every figure, sums `alreadyPaid` **excluding `status:VOID` payments** (P5-12 bounced checks must not count as paid), `applyAmount = roundMoney(min(remaining, invoiceBalance, requestedAmount))`, writes one `CREDIT_NOTE` `InvoicePayment` + bumps `amountUsed` + stamps `appliedAt`/`autoApplied` + flips `APPLIED` only when exhausted; returns `{applied:0}` (never throws) so callers can skip. `applyToInvoice` (public) keeps its own `Serializable` tx + all guards (expired/APPLIED/VOID/customer-mismatch/invoice-status) + throws on 0, delegates the money to `applyCreditInTx`. **`autoApplyOldestCreditsInTx(tx, invoiceId, customerId)`** — **PUBLIC** (cross-service call from `InvoicesService`; the only reason it's public — `applyCreditInTx` stays private), oldest `createdAt` first, `where` filters `status != VOID` + `OR expiresAt null/gt now` (Prisma can't compare two columns, so the `remaining > 0.001` half is a JS filter after fetch); loops credits passing the **running invoice balance as `requestedAmount`** — a hard clamp so it can never over-apply even on a stale re-read; idempotent (re-send finds nothing to apply). One or the other, never both: a credit reduces the invoice AS a `CREDIT_NOTE` payment AND leaves the wallet via `amountUsed` — never separately.
- **Regulated TYPE vs Category model (2026-07-19d):** UI-only rename — `TrackedCategory` = "Regulated type", `TrackedSubcategory` = the regulated item's "Category" (DB/API names unchanged). ONE category axis: `products.service` create/update SYNC `Product.category` to the structured category's name server-side (set → name wins incl. over dto.category; clear → null only when it was synced; category-only edits on structured products forced back); `tracked-categories` assign/unassign clear movers' synced categories; subcategory rename propagates to synced products (`category == oldName OR NULL`). Legacy tidy: `apps/api/scripts/tidy-regulated-categories.mjs` (dry-run default, `--tenant` via assertTestTenant, `--all-tenants --execute` = owner prod mode; **connection resolves like `prod-migrate.mjs` — `railway run --service postgres` provides POSTGRES\_\*/TCP-proxy PARTS, never DATABASE_URL, so the script builds the URL from parts, a set DATABASE_URL winning** — #303 fix after the documented command failed). Invoice-treatment labels ("Sectioned on invoice") deliberately untouched.
- **Credits ON ORDERS (2026-07-18, money-critical)** — new `OrderCreditNote` INTENT table (see schema entry) + service primitives: **`restoreCreditFromPaymentInTx`** (private, inverse of `applyCreditInTx` — deletes/shrinks the CREDIT_NOTE payment, decrements `amountUsed`, APPLIED→ISSUED, recomputes invoice status), **`validateSelectionsForCustomer`** (tenant/customer/VOID/expired/duplicate; deliberately does NOT require remaining>0 — idempotent resubmits), **`syncOrderCreditSelections(tx, orderId, customerId, selections|undefined)`** (undefined=untouched, []=remove all, else full-set diff; removed/amount-changed pairs un-apply first), **`settleOrderCreditsInTx(tx, orderId)`** (idempotent shrink-then-apply reconcile of intents → payments across the order's non-VOID invoices, base before -R# siblings; re-reads rows between passes — stale-snapshot under-apply was a caught+fixed review finding), **`unapplyFromInvoice`** (Serializable; restores pair + reduces/deletes the intent), **`updateCreditNote(id,{reason?,expiresAt?})`** (reason editable any status — makes reason edits real; expiresAt only ISSUED). `autoApplyOldestCreditsInTx` gained `opts.excludeCreditNoteIds` (send-time sweep skips EXPLICIT-amount order selections). Controller += `POST :id/unapply {invoiceId}` + `PATCH :id` (OPERATOR). Description renders AT READ TIME via `payments[].creditNote.reason` / `orderCreditNotes[].creditNote.reason` relations — never copied text. `prisma-mock.ts` += `orderCreditNote: modelProxy()`. Wiring: `createSplitInvoices` settles at the end of its creation tx (**payment rows stamp `tenantId` explicitly — fire-and-forget delivery path was tenant-orphaning them, caught+fixed**); `send`/`sendEmail` pass exclusions + **stuck-SENT fix** (fully-pre-credited draft now lands PAID via post-auto-apply recompute); `deletePayment`/`voidPayment` REFUSE CREDIT_NOTE (point at unapply); **ADVANCE orphan FIXED (follow-ups PR): both paths inline-restore `AdvancePayment.balance += payment.amount` in the same tx (advances have no status machinery — a balance increment is the complete inverse of `applyAdvancePaymentToInvoice`'s decrement); deletePayment's restore is gated `payment.status !== "VOID"` (void already restored — the void-then-delete double-credit was a caught+fixed review finding); mobile `paymentActionFlags(status, method?)` hides Void for CREDIT_NOTE**; orders: `AppliedCreditNoteDto[]` on create/sale/update-items DTOs (staff-gated on edit), create() validates up-front + post-commit sync+settle, **controller merge branch passes appliedCreditNotes through (silent-drop on merge was a caught+fixed review finding)**, updateOrderItems ALWAYS settles after reconcile routing (incl. partial-billing skip), changeStatus DELIVERED best-effort settle (warn-only; send catches up).
  - ⚠️ **F07/B108 correction (2026-09-01):** "send catches up" was FALSE as written. `send`/`sendEmail` exclude EXPLICIT-amount order selections from `autoApplyOldestCreditsInTx` (that is the `excludeCreditNoteIds` above), so the documented catch-up deliberately skipped exactly the credits a swallowed delivery settle was responsible for — an operator-attached credit with an explicit amount could be applied by nothing. Both methods now call **`settleOrderCreditsInTx(tx, updated.orderId)` BEFORE computing `explicitIds`**, which makes the catch-up real; the exclusion is deliberately KEPT so the oldest-first sweep still cannot override an explicit amount, and settle clamps so money never moves twice. The delivery settle itself retries 3× (100/300ms) and logs at ERROR on final failure — delivery still never fails on it.
- **CANCEL / VOID / DELETE now give the credit back (2026-08-13, money-critical)** — a credit applied to an order was consumed FOREVER once the order was cancelled: cancel voided only the pending DRAFT (`findOpenOrderDraft`), the CREDIT_NOTE payments survived on the dead invoice with `amountUsed` still up, and `settleOrderCreditsInTx` filters VOID invoices out, so no path could ever return it. `deleteOrder` was worse — it hard-deleted those payment rows without decrementing `amountUsed` (credit gone, no trace). A SENT invoice on a cancelled order also stayed live and collectible.
  New primitives: **`releaseOrderCreditsInTx(tx, orderId)`** / **`releaseInvoiceCreditsInTx(tx, invoiceId)`** over a shared private `releaseCreditsInTx(tx, invoiceWhere)` — restores every non-VOID CREDIT_NOTE payment in scope via `restoreCreditFromPaymentInTx`, **deliberately WITHOUT an invoice-status filter so already-VOID invoices are swept** (that filter is exactly what stranded the money; safe to re-run because each restore DELETES the payment row it consumed), and the order-scoped one also `deleteMany`s the `OrderCreditNote` intents so a later settle can't re-apply what was just returned. **`previewOrderCreditRelease(orderId)`** is its read-only twin for the confirmation UI. `restoreCreditFromPaymentInTx` now **REVIVES** a note whose `expiresAt` has passed (clears the stale expiry; a FUTURE expiry is untouched) — owner decision 2026-08-13, since money handed back to an expired note is invisible to every open-credit reader.
  Invoices: **`externalPaidOn(payments)`** splits WALLET money (CREDIT_NOTE/ADVANCE — returnable) from EXTERNAL money (cash/check/card — software can't un-take it); **`releaseWalletPaymentsInTx(tx, invoiceId)`** returns credits + re-credits `AdvancePayment.balance`; **`voidInvoiceInTx(tx, id, orderId)`** is the guard-free core (status→VOID, `adjustInvoicedQtyForInvoice(-1)`, ledger reverse). `voidInvoice` now blocks ONLY on external money (was: any PAID/PARTIAL) and releases wallet money first, so a credit-only-paid invoice is voidable instead of dead-ending.
  Orders: `changeStatus(CANCELLED)` calls **`assertCancellableOrThrow`** BEFORE the status write (a refused cancel leaves the order untouched), then in ONE Serializable tx releases credits and voids **every** live invoice. `deleteOrder` reuses the same guard + release. New **`cancelImpact(id)`** + `GET /orders/:id/cancel-impact` (OPERATOR) returns `{invoicesToVoid, creditsToRestore[{creditNoteNumber,amount}], advanceToRestore, blockingPayments, deliveredUnits, canCancel}` (**`deliveredUnits` added by F07/B56 — `canCancel` now also requires it be ≤ 0.001**) — the guard and the warning read the SAME function so they cannot disagree. Client copy lives in the `{web,mobile}/lib/cancel-impact.ts` mirrors (`describeCancelImpact`), whose `deliveredUnits` is deliberately **optional**: a client build can be older or newer than the API answering it, and an absent field must read as "no delivered goods" rather than crash the only warning shown before live invoices are voided. Specs: credit-notes (5 release + 1 preview), orders (void-all-invoices, credits-returned, cash-blocks-and-leaves-untouched).
  **NOT changed — open decision:** returns still mint a NEW credit note. Restoring the returned value onto the ORIGINAL note only balances if the invoice ALSO stops billing the returned goods; on its own it re-opens an invoice balance that auto-apply pushes the same dollars straight back into (customer re-billed). See `.personal/` handoff.

### `returns/`

- **controller** `returns` — `@Get/:id`, approve, reject, in-transit, receive, refund, cancel.
- **service** — `findAll`/`findAllForUser(userId,role)` (CUSTOMER filtered), `findOne`, `create`, `approve`, `reject`, `markInTransit`, `receive` (RETURN movement restocks at current averageCost with unitCost+snapshots stamped), `refund`, `cancel`. side effects: Return/ReturnItem writes; RETURN movement on receive; credit-note auto-gen on refund; email.
- **`create` over-return guard (B10)** — rejects a non-array/empty `dto.items` with 400 up front (`POST /returns` binds `@Body() dto: any`, so nothing validates the shape and the loop used to 500). Then, inside one `tenantTransaction`: `tx.$executeRaw SELECT id FROM "Order" … FOR UPDATE` **first** (tenantTransaction is READ COMMITTED — the tx alone does not serialize concurrent creates; mirrors `invoices.service.recordPayment`), then the `tx.return.findMany` cumulative read, per-line validation, and `tx.return.create`. Each validated line accumulates into `alreadyReturned` so one payload repeating a productId can't over-return. Spec: `returns-overreturn.spec.ts`.
- **`processRefund` → store-credit dispute (P5-13)** — flips RECEIVED→REFUNDED, then (sequentially, NOT nested — `CreditNotesService.create` opens its own Serializable tx) mints a **lump-sum** ISSUED `CreditNote` (**no line items** — the regulated ledger was already reversed at `receive()` via `reverseReturnEntries`; a line-linked credit would double-reverse) for `Σ item.qty × (orderLine.subtotal ÷ orderLine.qty)` (boxed-safe: `subtotal÷qty`, not `unitPrice`, since a box-priced line has `unitPrice` per box while `qty` is pieces; falls back to `unitPrice` if `qty` is 0), skips create on $0. Single source invoice → passed as `invoiceId` (engages the cumulative over-credit cap); 0 or 2+ invoices → standalone credit. Links via `Return.creditNoteId`. Injects `CreditNotesService` (`ReturnsModule` imports `CreditNotesModule` — acyclic, no `forwardRef`).

### `order-templates/`

- **controller** `order-templates` — get/patch/delete, items add/remove, `:id/generate`.
- **service** — `findAll`, `findOne`, `update`, `delete`, `addItem`, `removeItem`, `generateOrder` (draft Order from template). side effects: OrderTemplate(+Item)/Order writes. **Tax on `generateOrder` reads the TENANT's `settings.taxRate` per-request via `SystemConfigService` + `common/tax-rate.ts` `taxRateFractionFrom` — it used to be a constructor-cached `env TAX_RATE ?? 0.1`, i.e. a flat 10% regardless of tenant settings. `OrderTemplatesModule` therefore imports `SystemConfigModule` (without it the app fails to boot on the new constructor arg).**
- **DRIVER removed from every mutation (B133, F14 2026-09-02)** — `create`/`update`/`removeItem`
  admitted `@Roles(OPERATOR, CUSTOMER, DRIVER)` and `addItem` admitted
  `@Roles(OPERATOR, DRIVER)`; the driver screens that motivated the original grant (commit
  `028f86b0`) were deleted long ago, leaving driver-written template content (create/update/
  addItem/removeItem) writable by any driver token and then materialized into a BILLED order by
  the template's daily-cron `generateOrder` (06:00), which was already `[OPERATOR, CUSTOMER]` and
  never itself admitted DRIVER. DRIVER dropped from all four; the matrix is now `[OPERATOR,
CUSTOMER]` for create/update/removeItem (unchanged for generateOrder) and **`[OPERATOR]`** for
  `addItem` (CUSTOMER was never admitted there even before this fix). `findAll`/`findOne` carry no `@Roles` at all — a DRIVER
  can still LIST/READ templates; recorded as an intentional residual, not fixed here (out of
  scope — see `pin (B133)` in the roles spec). **`addItem` now delegates to a new ownership
  wrapper `addItemForUser(templateId, dto, user)`** (`service.addItem` itself is unchanged and
  now private in effect — the controller never calls it directly), which calls the existing
  `findOneForUser` ownership check (F2-003 — throws for a CUSTOMER who doesn't own the template)
  before delegating to `addItem`. For OPERATOR this is a pass-through today (no ownership branch
  fires), but it closes the door on a future CUSTOMER grant on `addItem` shipping unguarded —
  uniformity with its siblings `removeItemForUser`/`generateOrderForUser`, which already had this
  shape. Specs: `order-templates.controller.roles.spec.ts` (new — role-decorator matrix + the
  controller delegates to `addItemForUser` not `addItem`), `order-templates.service.spec.ts`
  (`addItemForUser` ownership cases).
- **REG-B48 (F13) — `generateOrder` prices through the SHARED buyer resolver.** Every template line
  used to bill raw `Number(product.pricePerUnit)`, so a standing order ignored the customer's
  pricing tier, per-product `CustomerPrice` overrides, active promotions (incl. `BUY_N_GET_M` free
  units) and the sticky-upsell price memory that every interactive order already applies. It now
  loads `customer.pricingTier`, the `CustomerPrice` rows for the line products (`pricingTier: null`
  = MSRP-only, falls through to the default tier), `ordersService.loadActivePromotions(CUSTOMER)`
  and `ordersService.getCustomerPriceHistory(customerId)`, then calls
  **`ordersService.resolveBuyerLinePrice(...)`** per line and `computeLineSubtotal({unitPrice, qty,
freeUnits})`; the line persists `priceType`/`originalPrice`/`promoFreeUnits` like the interactive
  path. A template qty is a SELLING-UNIT count, so `qtyPieces = qty × unitsPerBox` is passed for
  promo thresholds while `qty` still bills as whole units. **`OrdersService.loadActivePromotions`
  and `resolveBuyerLinePrice` were made public for this — they are now a shared contract, not
  orders-private.** Spec: `order-templates.pricing-and-items.spec.ts` (REG-B48 T9–T16, run against
  the REAL resolver and the REAL `computeLineSubtotal`); `order-templates.service.spec.ts` gains the
  matching `generateOrder` pins (the resolver is called once per line, tax still reads the tenant's
  `settings.taxRate`).
- **REG-B09 (F13) — `PATCH :id` can replace items.** `UpdateOrderTemplateDto` gains
  `items?: OrderTemplateItemDto[]` (`@IsArray @ArrayMinSize(1) @ValidateNested @Type`), reusing the
  now-**exported** `OrderTemplateItemDto` from `dto/create-order-template.dto.ts`; without the field
  the whitelist pipe silently 400'd/dropped the customer page's "Edit Standing Order" item edits.
  `update()` keeps the scalar-only path untouched and, when `items` is present, validates every
  `productId` in-tenant BEFORE any write, then does `orderTemplateItem.deleteMany` +
  `orderTemplate.update` with nested `create` (each row stamped with `tenantId`) inside ONE
  `tenantTransaction`, so a failure can never leave a template with zero items. `items: null` is
  treated as absent (class-validator skips every validator for null); `items: []` is a 400. Specs:
  `dto/update-order-template.dto.spec.ts` (REG-B09 T23 + whitelist pins — no client `unitPrice` on a
  template item), `order-templates.pricing-and-items.spec.ts` (REG-B09 T25/T26).

### `drafts/` (Minimize & resume, pos-cost-roles-spec §2)

- **controller** `drafts` — `/drafts` CRUD (`@Roles(OPERATOR, DRIVER)`; TENANT_ADMIN satisfies OPERATOR).
- **service** — `list`/`create`/`update`/`get`/`remove`; per-user ownership (`assertOwned`) + tenant-scoped (`forTenant()`); autosave upserts the same draft. Model **`SaleDraft`** (per-user, `payload` Json, additive migration `20260705120000_add_sale_drafts` — CREATE TABLE only; **apply to prod before the web dock deploys**). Spec `drafts.service.spec.ts`. Web: `components/DraftDock.tsx` + `CreateOrderModal` Minimize/resume.

### `inventory/`

- **controller** `inventory` — overview, movements (+purchase/adjustment), stock-count/commit, **valuation**, **`@Patch products/:id/cost-basis`**, **`@Post cost-basis/bulk`**, **`@Post recompute-costs`**, **`@Post variant-assign`** (PR-D, generic→variants), suppliers CRUD, purchase-orders CRUD + send/receive/close (list = `ListPurchaseOrdersDto`), forecasting.
- **DRIVER dropped from the four writes that still carried it (B168, F14 2026-09-02)** —
  `recordPurchase`/`recordAdjustment`/`createPO`/`receivePO` each had a handler-level
  `@Roles(OPERATOR, DRIVER)` that OVERRODE the class-level `@Roles(OPERATOR)` (`RolesGuard`
  resolves `getAllAndOverride(handler, class)` — a handler decorator always wins, never merges).
  Those four decorators are simply DELETED (not replaced), so the handlers now inherit the
  class-level OPERATOR-only gate. `commitStockCount`/`sendPO`/`closePO` already carried no
  handler-level override and needed no change — all seven inventory writes now resolve to exactly
  `[OPERATOR]`. The four inventory **reads** (`getStockOverview`, `listMovements`, `listPOs`,
  `getPO`) deliberately keep `DRIVER` — out of scope, pinned in the spec. Spec:
  `inventory.controller.roles.spec.ts` (new — asserts `getAllAndOverride` resolution per handler,
  not just the decorator's own metadata, since a containment check on the handler alone would
  pass even if DRIVER also sat on the class).
- ⚠️ **`recordSale` HAS NO CALLER on master and has not had one since `c5f579c2` (2026-07-16) dropped the `/orders/:id/complete` route** — it was wired only 2026-07-04→07-16. Consequences, all live: **zero `StockMovement type:"SALE"` rows exist**, so `bookkeeping.getProfitAndLoss` reports `cogs = 0` (gross profit = revenue) and five analytics/forecasting readers read an empty source (those readers were re-sourced to invoiced sales, cherry-picked from `claude/priceless-matsumoto-abc131` @ `eaa46ecc`). It is also the only writer that draws `StockLot.remainingQty` DOWN, so lots only ever grow while `orders.service.ts` decrements `Product.currentStock` directly — which used to inflate valuation for the 98% of products labelled FIFO — since fixed by valuing everything at the weighted average (see `getValuation` below). The system is AVCO on every write, and now on every read. Treat the entries below as describing code that exists, not code that runs.
- **service** — `getStockOverview`, `listMovements`, `recordPurchase`, `recordAdjustment`, `commitStockCount`, **`recordSale(productId,qty,ref,userId,tx)` — the sale-costing path, currently UNCALLED (see above)** (per-method unitCost: AVCO=avg, FIFO/LIFO=lot blend w/ avg fallback, STANDARD=standardCost, **LAST_COST=most recent PURCHASE StockMovement unitCost (typed; `orderBy [createdAt desc, id desc]`), avg fallback, not lot-consuming — MUST use the PURCHASE movement not the latest StockLot, since adjustments/stock-counts create avg-cost lots that would poison last-cost**; writes SALE movement WITH unitCost + snapshots, decrements stock, consumes lots, returns `{unitCost, stockAfter}`; never throws on negative stock). `CostingMethod` enum incl. **LAST_COST** (additive migration `20260706040000_add_costing_last_cost`; selectable per-product; tenant-default→product propagation = follow-on, QUESTIONS.md #10), PO CRUD + receive (Decimal AVCO), `getForecasting` (signed SALE sums), **`setCostBasis`/`bulkSetCostBasis`** (COST_BASIS movement qty 0 + product.averageCost; **also writes `standardCost` when the product is STANDARD** so the stock table clears its "no cost" state; optional applyToLots), **`recomputeCosts({productIds?,dryRun?})`** (replays movement history → rebuilds averageCost + backfills avgCostAfter/stockAfter snapshots; reports noHistory + stockDrift; **STANDARD products keep averageCost FROZEN — `replayProduct` mirrors `recordPurchase`'s no-average rule, so recompute/backdated-repair never fabricates a bogus correction**), **`getValuation`** + `getStockOverview`. **VALUATION is weighted-average for EVERY method (2026-08-09): the shared `effectiveValue` helper returns `averageCost` (STANDARD → `standardCost ?? averageCost`) and no longer reads stock lots — `openLotSums` is deleted. Lot-based FIFO/LIFO valuation was only sound while lots were drawn down, and `recordSale` has had no caller since c5f579c2, so lots only grew while stock fell and the read reported the cost of everything ever received (prod: +$12,463.06 over 54 products, 5 valued at zero stock). Every write already maintains `averageCost` via `nextAverageCost` regardless of label, so this makes the read match the write. `costingMethod` now defaults to AVCO (migration `20260809000000_costing_default_avco` sets only the column DEFAULT; existing rows keep their label, which no longer affects any number).** `commitStockCount` is **idempotent** — a re-posted `sessionId` (lost response / 502 retry) short-circuits on the existing `STOCK_COUNT-{sessionId}` movements (returns `{alreadyCommitted:true}`) instead of double-applying ADD deltas + duplicating lots. Cost/qty DTOs carry `@Max` bounds matching the Decimal(10,4)/(10,3) columns (overflow → 400, not a Prisma 500). Backdated purchase/adjustment auto-replays the product in-tx.
- **PR-C durable stock-count sessions (2026-08-20, MIGRATION `20260820000000_add_stock_count_sessions` — purely additive: 2 enums + 2 tables + indexes/FKs, no ALTER of an existing table):** new `StockCountSession` (OPEN|REVIEW|COMMITTED|DISCARDED, `startedById`, `committedById`, `movementReference`, `amendsSessionId`) + `StockCountLine` (`@@unique([sessionId, productId])`, `countedQty`/`boxes`/`pieces`, `expectedQty`, `unitCostOverride` 4dp, `countedById`, `mode` REPLACE|ADD). **Before this a count lived ONLY in the client (localStorage on web, component state on mobile) — `lib/api/stock-count.ts` literally said "there is no server-side session model"** — so it could not resume on another device and left no history. Routes: `POST|GET /inventory/stock-counts`, `GET|POST /stock-counts/:id` (+`/commit`, `/discard`), `PUT /stock-counts/:id/lines`, `DELETE /stock-counts/:id/lines/:productId`. **The pre-existing one-shot `POST /inventory/stock-count/commit` is UNCHANGED and still works.**
  - **Both commit paths share `applyStockCountItemsInTx`** (extracted) so stock semantics can never drift between them; both keep the `reference = STOCK_COUNT-<id>` idempotency guard, and the session path additionally short-circuits on `status === COMMITTED`.
  - **MONEY, load-bearing order:** a line's `unitCostOverride` writes the audited COST_BASIS path (`setCostBasisInTx`) **BEFORE** its quantity adjustment, and the adjustment re-reads the product inside the tx — otherwise the ADJUSTMENT's `avgCostAfter` and any StockLot opened for a positive delta would be stamped with the STALE cost. Spec pins the `["COST_BASIS","ADJUSTMENT"]` order and the lot's corrected unitCost.
  - `expectedQty` is snapshotted on the FIRST count of a line only, so the variance means "what changed since you started counting" rather than tracking stock moving under the counter. Uncounted products are NEVER touched. `increment:true` is the live-scan path (+1, deliberately NOT idempotent); edits send the absolute total. Boxes/pieces recompute qty via `normalizeBoxesPieces`. Amending a COMMITTED session opens a NEW pre-filled one that **re-snapshots `expectedQty` from CURRENT stock** and never rewrites history. A concurrent OPEN session is returned as `otherOpenSessions` — a WARNING, never a lock.
  - The LIST endpoint computes `netVarianceMoney` per session server-side (same delta rule as the commit, at each product's current `averageCost`, via `roundMoney`) and DROPS the raw lines from the payload — otherwise every history row would need its own detail fetch (N+1) to show its headline number, and web/mobile could drift from the server.
  - ⚠️ Lines are written ONE BY ONE (`stockCountLine.create`), never nested under the session create: a nested write bypasses the tenant extension and lands `tenantId = null`, invisible to `forTenant()`.
- **`costing.ts`** — pure Decimal helpers (same rounding discipline as @routeflow/pricing, 4dp): `costDecimal`, `nextAverageCost` (stock≤0 ⇒ reset to unitCost), `reverseAverageCost` (**null = KEEP previous avg** when reversal empties stock — never zero the basis), `planLotConsumption`. Spec: `costing.spec.ts`. ALL inventory writers must go through these.
- **StockMovement snapshots**: every movement now stamps `avgCostAfter`/`stockAfter` (product state AFTER) — point-in-time avg cost = latest movement ≤ T. `MovementType.COST_BASIS` = audited manual cost set (qty 0). Migration `20260704000000_inventory_cost_accounting`.
- side effects: StockLot/StockMovement/PurchaseOrder(+Item)/Product.averageCost writes; ledger. Specs: `inventory.service.spec.ts`.
- **PO list DTO (2026-08-25):** `dto/list-purchase-orders.dto.ts` — GET `/inventory/purchase-orders` previously took raw `query: any`, so `?limit=20` reached Prisma as the STRING `"20"` (prod `PrismaClientValidationError` on `take`). Now `@Type(() => Number)` + `@IsInt @Min(1)` on `page`/`limit` (defaults 1/20; no `limit=0` fetch-all sentinel callers exist — checked web+mobile), `@IsEnum(PurchaseOrderStatus) status`, `supplierId`, plus `from`/`to` whitelisted-but-NOT-filtered (the web POs tab sends them; global `forbidNonWhitelisted` would 400 the request otherwise — server-side date filtering still unimplemented). Spec: `dto/list-purchase-orders.dto.spec.ts`. ⚠️ mobile `useOpenPurchaseOrders` sends `status=PARTIALLY_RECEIVED`, which is NOT in the enum (`DRAFT|SENT|PARTIAL|RECEIVED|CLOSED`) — that call was already a Prisma 500, now a clean 400; the mobile client fix is a separate follow-up.
- **Receiving box→piece conversion (2026-08-23, branch fix/receiving-box-conversion — the client-stock-corruption fix):** stock is PIECES system-wide, but `recordPurchase` (Quick Restock) and `receivePurchaseOrder` wrote the RAW entered number — an operator thinking in boxes under-received by `unitsPerBox`, driving on-hand hugely negative. Now: `RecordPurchaseDto` gains optional `boxes`/`pieces` (`quantity` became optional but KEEPS meaning pieces when sent — never reinterpreted); both receive paths resolve `qtyPieces` once up front via `normalizeBoxesPieces` when the payload is explicitly boxed. `receivePurchaseOrder` also: REPLACED the silent `Math.min(receivedQty, maxReceivable)` clamp with a LOUD BadRequest (a box-denominated legacy `qtyOrdered` vs a piece receipt would silently under-receive and flip the PO to RECEIVED forever), Decimal-safe outstanding math, cost basis pinned to the STORED per-piece `item.unitCost` (the Create-PO web form converts cost-per-box→per-piece before posting; receive-payload shape never changes valuation). PO detail/list selects now include `product.unitsPerBox`. Web: Quick Restock + Create/Receive-PO collect boxes+pieces for boxed products (`inventory/page.tsx`), on-hand renders pieces + `(X boxes + Y pcs)` via shared `apps/web/lib/stock-label.ts` (`unitsLabel` tolerates non-finite → "—"). Mobile: `purchase-orders/record.tsx` + `[id]/receive.tsx` collect boxes+pieces (receive seeds the split from remaining via `normalizeBoxesPieces`). Read-only `apps/api/scripts/report-receiving-unit-drift.mjs` (assertTestTenant-guarded, no --execute) reconstructs suspected under-received products per tenant for the owner-approved data repair. ⚠️ `qtyOrdered` on POs created via mobile/pre-cutover may be box-denominated — receiving them now FAILS LOUDLY with guidance instead of corrupting.
- **P5-03 stock-alert fire hooks:** `InventoryModule` imports `StockAlertsModule`. `recordPurchase`/`recordAdjustment`(qty>0 only)/`commitStockCount`(count-up products)/`receivePurchaseOrder`(actually-received items) each call the service's private `fireStockAlerts(productIds)` AFTER their `tenantTransaction` resolves (never inside it, try/catch, non-throwing) — see `buyer/` `stock-alerts/ StockAlertService` for the fire semantics.
- **PR-D WP1 — `POST inventory/variant-assign` / `assignToVariants` (2026-08-20, no migration):** atomically moves stock from a generic parent `Product` to its variants inside ONE `tenantTransaction` at **`isolationLevel:"Serializable"`** (the pool check is a read-then-write invariant; READ COMMITTED let two concurrent splits both pass and drive the parent negative). "Unassigned stock" = the parent's own `currentStock`, not new state. DTO `VariantAssignDto` (`dto/variant-assign.dto.ts`): `parentProductId` + `assignments[]` (each: existing `productId` OR inline `newVariant:{name}`, plus `qty` or `boxes`/`pieces`, optional `unitCostOverride`). Flow: load parent (404; 400 `"A variant cannot itself be split"` if the parent is itself a variant) → resolve each assignment's qty (`boxes`/`pieces` win via `normalizeBoxesPieces` against the PARENT's `unitsPerBox`; qty≤0 dropped; **400 if `boxes`/`pieces` are sent for a parent with `unitsPerBox`≤1** — `normalizeBoxesPieces` would resolve those to 0 and silently drop the row) → **pool check INSIDE the tx** against a freshly-read `parent.currentStock` (400 `{code:"INSUFFICIENT_UNASSIGNED"}`, writes nothing — this is what makes two concurrent splits of the same generic safe) → resolve every target BEFORE any write (existing `productId` must be `findFirst({id, parentProductId, isActive:true})` — an **ACTIVE** child of THIS parent, 400 otherwise, since a deactivated variant is invisible to every sellable surface; a new-variant name collision also 400s; **a duplicate `productId` or repeated new-variant name 400s too** — both rows would be costed/stock-stamped off the same pre-write snapshot) → draw the parent's `StockLot`s down for the TOTAL via `planLotConsumption` (always, NOT gated on costingMethod like `recordSale` — a transfer must conserve lots for every costing label; **only the COVERED quantity is re-lotted** — `lotPlan.uncovered` (a generic whose opening stock was imported straight onto `currentStock` has no lots) is left un-lotted rather than invented on the variant side) → one negative `ADJUSTMENT` on the parent for the total (parent's `averageCost` is NEVER touched) + one positive `ADJUSTMENT` per variant + one new `StockLot` per variant (for its covered share), all sharing `reference = "VARIANT_ASSIGN-"+randomUUID()` → variant cost = `unitCostOverride ?? parentEffectiveCost` where **`parentEffectiveCost` = the same rule as `effectiveValue()`** (`STANDARD ? standardCost ?? averageCost : averageCost`) — reading the raw `averageCost` column alone moved a STANDARD generic (whose `averageCost` is legitimately null, `recordPurchase` never writes it) at $0; `nextAverageCost` applies only when `variant.costingMethod !== STANDARD` (mirrors `recordPurchase`'s guard) **and a cost is actually known** — with no cost anywhere the variant's `averageCost` is left untouched rather than stamped 0 (a fabricated 0 reads as a real cost in `getValuation` and drops off the "no cost set" report) → parent/variant `currentStock` updated → `fireStockAlerts` on variants that gained stock, after commit. **A new inline variant is NOT created via `ProductsService.create()`** — `InventoryModule` importing `ProductsModule` was out of WP1's file scope, so `InventoryService.createVariantRowInTx` hand-rolls the SAME parent-inheritance fields `create()` would have applied (price tiers, category, isTobacco, costingMethod, standardCost, unitsPerBox, regulated set) directly via `tx.product.create`, plus a matching SKU auto-gen (`generateVariantSku`, same 3-letter+4-hex algorithm as `products.service.ts`). If `ProductsModule` is later wired into `InventoryModule`, this hand-rolled path could be replaced by a real `ProductsService.create()` call. Spec: `variant-assign.spec.ts` (INSUFFICIENT_UNASSIGNED writes-nothing, partial assignment, paired-movement/reference sharing, non-child target 400, variant-of-variant 400, boxed boxes+pieces conversion, unitCostOverride vs parent-average fallback, STANDARD averageCost guard, lot conservation, new-variant inheritance, STANDARD-parent standardCost costing, no-cost-anywhere leaves averageCost alone, ACTIVE-child-only target, duplicate-target 400, boxes-on-unboxed-parent 400, uncovered-lot capping).
- **PR-D WP2 — vendor-bill line "has variants?" hint:** `vendor-bills.service.findOne` (ONLY there — not the list, not the write sites) widens its line-item `product` select with `parentProductId` + `_count:{select:{variants:{where:{isActive:true}}}}` so the bill detail page can offer a "Generic — split into variants?" badge. **ACTIVE-only count** so the badge matches what `variant-assign` will actually accept as a target. Additive fields only. Spec: `vendor-bills-variant-hint.spec.ts`.

### `billing/`

- **controller** `billing` (+ `billing/webhook`) — tenant billing get, checkout, portal, ensure-customer; Stripe webhook.
- **service** — `initializeCheckout`, `createCustomerPortal`, `ensureStripeCustomer`, `handleWebhook`. `AddonService.hasAddon/getActiveAddons/enable/disable` = tenant feature flags (TenantAddon).
- **`addon.guard.ts` + `require-addon.decorator.ts`** — `@RequireAddon(...keys)` + AddonGuard. **GOTCHA: guards run BEFORE TenantInterceptor — AddonGuard must read `req.user.tenantId`, never `prisma.getTenantId()`** (ALS empty). Order: `@UseGuards(JwtAuthGuard, RolesGuard, AddonGuard)`. SUPER_ADMIN (null tenantId) passes. Tenant-facing flag read: `GET /tenants/me/addons` (OPERATOR + DRIVER).
  - **Variadic / any-of (2026-08-28):** `RequireAddon = (...addonKeys: string[]) => SetMetadata(REQUIRE_ADDON_KEY, addonKeys)` — metadata is now a `string[]`; the guard still accepts the LEGACY single string (`typeof raw === "string" ? [raw] : raw`) so untouched call sites keep working. Resolution is ONE `AddonService.getActiveAddons(tenantId)` round-trip + `keys.some(...)`, not a `hasAddon` per key. The 403 message names only PURCHASABLE keys — `INTERNAL_ADDON_KEYS = new Set(["developer_mode"])` is honoured as an any-of key but filtered out of the text (the web toasts `ForbiddenException.message` verbatim, so echoing a hidden platform-admin flag would leak it); wording falls back to the single-key phrasing when one purchasable key remains, and to "This feature is not enabled for your account." when none do. Handler metadata WINS over class metadata (`getAllAndOverride` checks the handler first) — that's how `TripsController.updatePlanning` widens its own gate.
  - **`developer_mode` (2026-08-20, NARROWED 2026-08-28)** is a legacy free-text `TenantAddon.addonKey` (`DEVELOPER_MODE_ADDON` in `@routeflow/types`) toggled from the platform-admin tenant page. It now unlocks ONLY genuinely in-development surfaces — the mobile `(driver)` app, the mobile role-picker driver option, the mobile `(tenant)` dispatch tab. It **no longer unlocks the two GA delivery addons in any client UI** (web/mobile gates read `useRoutesAccess`/`useDeliveryAccess`, which are the feature addon alone). `plan-catalog.constants.ts` carries the pending bridge note (a future DEV_MODE AddonSku granting `flag.dispatch_live`); the mapping is NOT in `LEGACY_ADDON_KEY_TO_SKU` because no such SKU exists yet.
  - **Dispatch API gate (2026-08-28, replaces the old "deliberately ungated" stance):** the dispatch surface IS server-enforced now, mirroring the `driver_payments` precedent. Class-level `@UseGuards(JwtAuthGuard, …, AddonGuard)` + `@RequireAddon(...)` on **five** controllers — `RoutesController` + `RouteRunsController` (`routes.controller.ts`), `DriversController`, and route-optimization's `RouteOptimizationController` + `RouteTemplateOptimizationController` all take the EITHER-gate `("recurring_routes", "order_delivery", "developer_mode")`; `TripsController` takes `("order_delivery", "developer_mode")`. Rationale: `/routes`, `/route-runs`, `/drivers` and optimization are SHARED (an ad-hoc delivery materializes a route template + run), so a delivery-only tenant must keep them; `/trips` is the ad-hoc builder alone. **Exception:** `PATCH /trips/routes/:routeId/planning` (`updatePlanning`) carries its OWN either-gate — it is saved from the shared run-detail page a recurring-routes-only tenant can reach. `developer_mode` is accepted server-side purely so a dev tenant can drive the in-dev mobile driver app end-to-end. Wiring: `trips.module.ts`, `drivers.module.ts`, `route-optimization.module.ts` gained `BillingModule` (routes.module.ts already had it).
